2026年 10月 29日

加息暂停信息中性，等待地缘局势发展的更多信号 
————2026年 10月份美联储议息会议点评 
黄恬  期货投资咨询证号：Z0021089   韩泽文 期货投资咨询号 Z0021517         
事件：美东时间 10月 28日周三，美联储公布，货币政策委员会 FOMC会议决定，将
联邦基金利率的目标区间维持在 3.75%至 4.00%不变。在 9月份决定三年来首次加息后，
此次暂停行动。虽然当前保持了耐心偏鸽，但这并不代表今年不会再加息，通过会后声明对
于经济强劲表述以及金融条件相关的表述，释放了偏鹰的信息。因此总的来看，传递的综合
信号是中性的。决议和发布会之后，会后市场的走势来看，美债短端利率回落，长端小幅回
升，美元小幅回落，黄金小幅反弹。 
点评：从会前关注来看：首先，暂停加息的政策定位。如果维持利率不变，沃什需要向
市场解释理由，并说明此举与推动 2%通胀目标回归的一致性。本次会议不更新点阵图，声
明和发布会对暂停理由的解释将更加重要；其次，美联储对就业风险的判断是否发生变化。9
月新增就业明显放缓，但失业率尚在区间波动，委员会是否将其视为需求走弱的早期信号，
将影响其愿意为压低通胀承担就业风险的程度。最后，美联储如何评估当前金融条件的实际
收紧效果。9月会议纪要指出，尽管长期美债收益率上升，股指上涨以及总体偏宽松的信贷
条件仍在支持经济增长。如果美联储维持这一判断，市场利率上行或不足以替代进一步政策
加息；如果认为融资成本已更广泛地传导至需求端，暂停加息的依据则会增强。因此，10月
即使暂停，也未必改变年内继续加息的方向，关键在于美联储认为货币政策还需要多少时间
以及多大力度，才能推动通胀持续回落。

### Page 2

- 2 -

美联储公布，货币政策委员会 FOMC会议决定，将联邦基金利率的目标区间维持在
3.75%至 4.00%不变。与 9月份 12名票委的全票赞成决议不同，本次会议 12名票委其中
有两名反对这一决议，认为应该加息。从本次会后声明来看，关于经济与政策的表达相较于
9月份议息会议做出了部分调整：首先，利率决议方面，调整为“维持目标区间在 3.75%—
4.00%”，反映本次会议暂停加息；其次，在经济问题的表述，将经济活动扩张速度从“稳
健（solid）”上调为“强劲（strong）”。有关地缘政治不确定性、国内支出韧性、生产率
增长、资本投资以及就业和失业率的表述均未改变；最后，在通胀与后续政策表述方面，美
联储继续表示通胀处于高位，但删除了 9月声明中“今天的政策行动将有助于更及时地实现
委员会 2%的目标”，改为强调“积累更多信息将帮助委员会确定适当的政策立场”，以确
保通胀及时回归 2%目标。声明仍重申，美联储将实现价格稳定。总的来看，提出暂停加息
是为了“积累更多信息”来辅助未来政策判断，则体现出了一定的“耐心”，这一点偏鸽。
但对于经济增长调整为“强劲”的表述，也体现了沃什强调近期货币政策对于经济的正向作
用，这一点偏鹰。因此整体来看属于偏中性的一个信号传递。 
从沃什的发布会内容来看：第一，关于议息会议的改革机制，美联储将从 2027年开始减
少利率制定会议的次数。八次会议虽然合适，但从 27年开始将逐步增加讨论货币政策战略的
会议次数， 减少仅聚焦于短期利率决策的会议。 同时沃什再度重申对于前瞻性指引的保留立场，
称除非在危机的特殊情况下；第二，关于 AI资本开支和经济基本面的“强劲”，他指出今年
上半年 GDP增速已从此前估计的 1.8%上修至 2.4%，第三季度增速看似进一步加快，沃什表
示近期 FOMC 讨论"主要集中在物价稳定职责上"。同时就业方面情况"相当积极"，失业率较
一年前下降，失业保险申请的四周均值处于"几代人以来最低水平"；第三，关于金融条件的限
制性问题，沃什表示他不会将当前金融条件整体定义为“紧张”，而是形容其有助于开辟通往

### Page 3

- 3 -

价格稳定的路径，对于 AI而言，他表示利率上升已经产生了“让部分资产的信用利差有所扩
大”以及“有些资产金融条件收紧”的迹象，尽管如此，他表示整体资本支出的故事"不仅强
劲，而且正在扩散"；最后，在美联储的政治独立性方面，沃什表示中期选举（定于11月 3日）
的政治因素对于本次会议决定“没有任何作用”。 
总的来看， 此次会议在暂停加息后， 从点阵图、 经济数据预测和发布会等方面的信息来看，
仍然传递了相对偏鹰派的信号：美联储强调经济的韧性，淡化加息对于供给侧冲击效果不明显
的言论，淡化目前收益率上行的冲击，并且继续强调对于通胀而言不能只看时点数据而要看趋
势，同时此次会议的表决联储委员的态度显著一致。这些信号表明，相较于加息和利率抬升带
来的负面因素而言，目前沃什仍然认为对于经济的冲击相对可控，继续坚守管理通胀这一目标
仍然是当前的首要任务。决议和发布会之后，美国 10 年期及两年期国债收益率均出现上涨，
美元指数走强，现货黄金跳水，美股由涨转跌。 
总的来看，结合着暂停加息的行动，以及声明和沃什发布会的内容，本次会议传递出相对
中性的信号：虽然此次会议暂停加息，表达了更多耐心，但是对于经济增长强劲的表述，表明
近期的利率抬升仍然没有对于经济产生负面的压力，并没有降低 12月份继续加息的预期。同
时在金融条件的表述上， 美联储虽然看到了一些收紧现象， 但是AI投资仍然处在扩张状态中，
表达出了对于这种边际上的收紧相对淡定的姿态，即金融体系对这种收紧的承受力仍然较高的
态度。决议和发布会之后，从市场的走势来看，美债短端利率回落，长端小幅回升，美元小幅
回落，黄金小幅反弹。 
展望未来 1-2个月，可以从以下几个方面把握宏观的主要矛盾的演化: 首先，地缘局势的
发展仍然在重要窗口期。进入 10月份之后，重要的地缘事件密集地汇集，俄乌谈判传来积极
信号，11 月初的谈判前也有停火，另一面则是特朗普对伊朗仍然强硬，胡塞武装和沙特的冲
突也尚未结束，因此目前的悬念仍然是 11月中上旬能否演化出俄乌和美伊的双向和平甚至停

### Page 4

- 4 -

火，这对于后续的大类资产交易的主线较为关键。从能源的角度来看，由于中东原油的出口已
经有明显恢复，“量”的压力已经有所解除，但是由于地缘溢价的因素，“价”的压力仍然较
为明显。沃什在 10月底议息会议上并没有给出更多货币政策的未来指引，但也释放出目前的
金融条件紧缩下，经济和金融市场仍然能够承受的信号，那么显然如果地缘局势仍然焦灼或者
有所升级，10 月份以来的美元偏强和美债信用利差抬升的趋势会延续，而如果地缘局势有缓
和的话，那么能源的“价”的压力缓和会压制通胀溢价，并缓和继续收紧的金融条件。目前来
看如何发展仍不明朗，需要继续跟踪； 
其次，关注中期选举结果以及结果确定后美伊战争的发展。进入 11月中旬后，APEC深
圳会议中美俄三方领导人能否会晤是后续的一个重要看点。 在这之前中期选举落地结果以及在
这之后是否仍然有美针对伊朗的军事行动较为关键。最差情景是共和党丧失两院叠加选举后美
伊仍然有军事冲突，最好情景则是共和党守住了参议院同时选举后伊核谈判落地。对于风险偏
好后续的预判应在 11月第一周根据发展相机抉择。    
最后，继续跟踪国内经济政策的发展思路，近期美联储带来的紧缩效应不仅让美国的金融
条件收紧，同时也让离岸美元流动性压力增加之下，大中华区的金融条件也在收紧。因此国内
权益市场的估值也因为这种金融条件的收缩而出现了快速的回落，但国内 Q3的 GDP增速整
体稳健。前者是金融市场的压力过度悲观地定价了基本面，后者是国内政策根据外围的不确定
性目前也保持一定的定力。因此，后续如果外围局势好转，那么如果有国内金融条件修复的时
间窗口，这种悲观预期将会矫正。反之则是金融市场的压力会逐步体现在 Q4基本面上。 
大类资产来看，从 9月份议息会议到此次会议前，基础格局是美元强势，同时美债利率强
势后引发全球的信用收缩，目前来看，10 月底的议息会议释放了中性的信号，近期外围市场
也有金融条件冲击小幅缓和的信号。后续来看，地缘局势发展是判断这种强美元和信用收缩压
力继续深化还是缓和的关键。后续无非是两条演化路径：

### Page 5

- 5 -

目前提防的仍然是能源压力缓解难，美债紧缩压力向股市和经济的进一步传导；伺机交易
的是能源压力缓和之下，信用紧缩和衰退压力的缓解以及美元流动性压力的缓和，给国内政策
带来的空间以及权益市场带来的估值修复。 
当前权益和商品指数的情绪都在较低位置，整体来看继续做空的性价比在大幅下降，但驱
动是否反转仍需更多信号。国内权益市场当前阶段，有“政策底”的预期，但仍然需要“政策
底”的标志性事件，而“市场底”的驱动仍然需要外部局势不确定性的落地，目前来看，进一
步下探的空间或有限，但也不能忽视尾部风险。从时间上来看，11 月上旬还是下旬之后才能
确认反弹成立尚待观察，配置上先以消费和金融等为主导，等待科技的下跌动能进一步消耗。
由于 11月中下旬中美俄元首会晤值得交易，因此可以通过牛市价差或者买虚值看涨的模式进
行参与。 
对于商品市场而言， 仍然处在多能化空国内需求侧商品的阶段， 不过能化目前整体在高位，
赔率有所下降，是否能够继续空中加油也需要根据地缘局势的演化而定。而9月下旬以后，很
多内需型产能过剩品种也已经有了一轮流畅的大幅下跌， 目前也在受到成本或者供给收缩的支
撑。后续来看，如果朝着地缘局势难缓解金融条件继续收缩的方向发展，那近期强势抗跌品种
可能出现补跌；而如果地缘局势向积极方向转变伴随金融条件的修复，那么金融属性强的有色
品种将出现反弹，并可能逐步伴随内需型商品的筑底。总体来看短期目前应聚焦于截面策略以
强弱对冲攻守相济为主，方向策略等待更为明确的指引。

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

Background: Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT), the world’s largest PCB 
manufacturer by revenue, generated revenue of TWD182.5bn in 2025. 
Historically focused on consumer electronics and smartphones, the 
company is shifting towards higher-value AI-server PCBs (HLC/iHDI), 
optical-module PCBs (mSAP), and IC substrates (ABF/BT). These 
businesses accounted for 21.6% of its revenue in 1H26, up from 11.7% in 
2025. Management targets this ratio to reach c.25% in 2026 and c.30% in 
2027. To support this shift, ZDT has 22 fabs under construction and plans 
to build nine more, primarily for mSAP/HDI, HLC, and ABF substrates.

Highlights: ZDT’s “One ZDT strategy” is gradually bearing fruit. Since 
2018, management has pursued its “One ZDT strategy” to broaden the 
product portfolio – including HLC, substrates, mSAP/HDI, rigid-flex and 
SLP – by leveraging its strengths in FPCB and consumer HDI. This 
strategy is beginning to pay off, and management expects its fast-growing 
segments, including mSAP HDI for optical transceivers, AI-server HDI and 
ABF/BT substrates, to gain momentum from this year. Management 
expects revenue from these segments to increase by c.150% YoY and 
account for c.25% of total revenue (or c.TWD50bn) in 2026, and to further 
increase by 100% YoY to TWD100bn in 2027. ZDT targets these fast-
growing segments to reach 30% of its revenue mix in 2027 and c.45-50% 
in 2030. Given current progress, the 2030 target could be reached in 2028. 
For mSAP HDI, management expects the 800G/1.6T transceiver product 
mix to shift to 7:3 in 2027, from 5:5 in 2026, and aims to become the largest 
supplier of optical-transceiver HDI in 2027. In AI-server HDI, we expect the 
2 leading ASIC vendors to gradually shift from pure HLC to thick HDI 
boards – a combination of HLC and HDI – in their next-generation designs, 
with ZDT a potential beneficiary. This could drive c.3x growth in ZDT’s 
business in 2027.

Product mix to support gross margin expansion. Margin expansion is 
increasingly driven by a favourable product mix. Gross margin reached 
22.4% in 1H26 (vs. 16.5% in 1H25). Management indicated that optical-
transceiver HDIs currently have the highest gross margin, at over 40%, 
followed by IC substrates, with ABF margins potentially reaching 50-60% 
and BT margins around 30% as a long-term target. Server HLC margins 
are around 20-30%, while HDI margins should be higher given their greater 
process complexity. With margins in traditional businesses at c.20%, ZDT 
expects growth in the newer segments to drive further margin expansion.

Valuation: The stock is trading at 36/19x PER for 2026/27E based on 
Bloomberg consensus. It’s past 3-year PER range is 10-30x.

20 October 2026

Business transformation bears fruit

➢ Key beneficiary of growth in optical transceiver & AI server HDI demand  
➢ Aggressive substrate capacity strategy paying off in current conditions   
➢ Margin expansion on fast-growing segments (HDI and substrates)

Share price performance

### Page 1

Nien Made Enterprise

3Q preview: EPS +13% YoY to NT$6.6; 
Better 2H but not yet a turnaround

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 441.00 TWD | Price: 358.50 TWD

3Qe EPS NT$6.6: strong rev and GP offset by opex and FX 
Nien Made is scheduled to report 3Q26 results on 4 Nov. Disclosed monthly sales imply 
3Q26 revenue growth of 16% YoY to NT$8.5bn, or 9% in USD terms, largely 
volume-driven. Growth reaccelerated from 1.7% YoY in 1H26, led by custom-made on a 
low US base and expansion in Europe and Japan. Ready-made also improved as 
Cambodia labor shortages eased. (See Exhibit 1 for US window coverings spending 
trends). Gross margin: We expect underlying GPM to rise 1.1ppt YoY to 59.8% on lower 
tariffs, broadly flat QoQ. We forecasts an NT$300mn of IEEPA tariff refunds in 2H26. 
Assuming NT$150mn is booked in 3Q, this would add 1.7ppt and lift reported GPM to 
61.5%. We expect the selling expense ratio to fall 1.7ppt QoQ to 17.5% on lower fuel 
charges, but remain 0.5ppt higher YoY due to continued salesforce and distribution 
network investment. We expect an FX loss in 3Q26 vs. an NT$118mn gain in 3Q25, 
weighing on YoY earnings growth. We forecast 3Q26 EPS of NT$6.6, up 13% YoY. 
US window covering picking up, but no turnaround yet  
BofA credit/debit card data suggests US spending on window coverings turned positive 
in Aug-26 for the first time after 19 consecutive months of YoY declines. Spending rose 
4%/3% YoY in Aug/Sep, respectively. This improvement partly explains the recent 
reacceleration in Nien Made's monthly sales. That said, we view the improvement more 
as normalization off a low base than the start of an industry turnaround. The Aug-Sep 
2025 comparison base was relatively weak, while the current rate-hike environment 
remains a headwind to housing-related demand. 
Slightly raise FY26 EPS est. to NT$22.9; PO to NT$441 
We raise our FY26 EPS estimate by 2% to NT$22.9 to reflect the blended impact of 
stronger-than-expected revenue growth and FX loss. We maintain our FY26 DPS forecast 
of NT$17 per share. We raise our PO by 4% to NT$441, as we roll forward our valuation 
basis from FY26E EPS to FY27E EPS. At the same time, we lower our target P/E from 
19x to 17x, reflecting the MSCI removal and a less supportive rate environment.

Key Metrics      
* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 7.

Company Sector 
Interior Furnishings

Company Description 
Nien Made, founded in 1974, is one of the world's largest 
window-covering manufacturers. It has roughly 50% 
market share in both ready-made (OEM business) shades 
and blinds, and custom-made (own brand) shutters in the 
US. The US market accounted for over 75% of sales. Key 
clients include Home Depot and Wal-Mart for ready-made 
shades and blinds. Nien Made operates its own retail 
network and e-commerce business for its custom-made 
shutters under the Norman brand name in the US and 
Europe.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on Nien Made, mainly given 1) clear 
custom-made growth profile (share gain in the US and fast-
growing DTC in Japan and Europe), 2) its largest customer 
Home Depot is gaining market share, and 3) margin 
expansion potentials backed by favorable products mix.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Nien Made Enterprise (XNRBF) 
Our PO NT$441 is derived from 17x P/E 2027E EPS. This is around 0.5SD above 
historical the one-year forward P/E, which we think is warranted given the visibility of 
the growth profile.

Downside risks are 1) weaker-than-expected Home Depot's sell-through, 2) slow 
recovery of US housing turnover, and 3) higher tariff increases on China exports to the 
US. 4) Loss of key customers.

iQmethod SMis the set of BofA Global Research standard measures that serve to maintain global consistency under three broad headings: Business Performance, Quality of Earnings, and validations. The key features of 
iQmethod are: A consistently structured, detailed, and transparent methodology. Guidelines to maximize the effectiveness of the comparative valuation process, and to identify some common pitfalls. 
iQdatabase ®  is our real-time global research database that is sourced directly from our equity analysts’ earnings models and includes forecasted as well as historical data for income statements, balance sheets, and cash 
flow statements for companies covered by BofA Global Research.

### Page 1

Summary: Ardentec released its 3Q26 results and held its investor meeting after market 
hours on 27 October. EPS of TWD3.1 beat our estimate (TWD3.04) and the consensus 
(TWD2.89) by 2.1% and 7.3%, respectively. Operating profit was broadly in line with our 
forecast, but beat consensus by 5%, driven by the Longtan plant ramp-up and higher 
utilisation rates.

We have a Buy (1) rating and 12M TP of TWD395, based on target PER of 25x on our 2027E 
EPS.

What’s the impact:

• Stronger-than-expected 3Q26 gross margin: Ardentec’s 3Q26 revenue reached 
TWD5,337m (+17.7% QoQ, +48.5% YoY), broadly in line with both our estimate and the 
consensus. The strong revenue growth was primarily driven by volume ramp-up at the 
Longtan facilities and supported by spillover demand. Gross margin came in at 45.4% 
(+5.2ppt QoQ, +9.7ppt YoY), beating our forecast and consensus expectations by 0.3ppt 
and 1.7ppt, respectively, mainly due to the Longtan plant ramp-up and higher utilisation 
rates (approximately 80%). Operating income was TWD1,860m (+35.0% QoQ, +112.8% 
YoY), largely in line with our estimate, but beating the consensus by 5.1%. Net profit of 
TWD1,473m, largely in line with both our estimate and the consensus. Although 3Q26 
EPS came in at TWD3.1, beating our estimate and the consensus by 2.1% and 7.3%, 
respectively, the variance was mainly due to share-dilution timing instead of operational 
issues.

• 4Q26 guidance largely in-line: Management guided for 4Q26 revenue to be flat or 
slightly up QoQ on traditional seasonality, which is in line with our forecast (up 3% 
QoQ). On margins, management guided for a slight QoQ decline in gross margin, 
mainly due to higher labour cost and depreciation expenses, which is also consistent 
with our forecast. We estimate 4Q26 gross margin at 44.0%, down 1.4ppt QoQ.

• Solid demand for 2027: For 2027, we expect Ardentec to continue benefiting from the 
AI-driven growth trend, supported by (1) robust wafer sorting outsourcing demand from 
tier-1 foundry customers and (2) strong AI networking demand from a major U.S. 
networking customer. The company is currently engaged in several co-packaged optics 
(CPO) projects with leading U.S. industry players, which could provide it long term 
opportunities and a larger addressable market.

Document 145: 261029_daiwa_Delta

Source PDF: all report pdf/261029_daiwa_Delta.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We provide our latest views on Delta after its 3Q26 results 
call on 29 October.

What’s the impact: Business outlook. During the results call, Delta 
remained positive on AI monetisation after its analysis of Anthropic’s S-1 
filing. Management flagged shortages of components, power and skilled 
electricians and slower-than-expected data centre buildout to be potential 
hiccups in the process; however, it still sees strong underlying demand. As 
such, we still expect sequential revenue growth in both 4Q26E and 1Q27E 
as component shortages had deferred 3Q26 demand. On rising material 
costs, Delta noted that BoM costs have increased by 2% as of 3Q26 (vs. 
early 2026), with the company gradually passing these costs through to 
end customers in 4Q26 and 1Q27. We believe the main gross margin 
driver will likely still be the absence of inventory write-offs and a higher 
power mix in 4Q26E. We forecast a gross margin recovery to 36% and a 
record operating margin of 18.5% in 4Q26. As for long-term growth, we 
maintain our investment theses that DC-DC module and HVDC adoption 
will become key growth drivers for Delta in 2027 and 2028, respectively.

3Q26 results missed on gross margin. Delta's 3Q26 earnings were 
TWD23.5bn (EPS: TWD9.04), missing our estimate of TWD27.3bn and 
Bloomberg consensus estimate of TWD29.6bn by 14% and 21%, 
respectively. We attribute the weakness mainly to lower-than-expected 
gross margin, driven by: 1) higher-than-expected inventory write-offs amid 
product shipment hiccups; 2) a one-off provision for customer claims, with 
the related loss likely to ultimately be borne by the supplier; and 3) rising 
material costs. Based on our calculation, Delta recognised c.TWD4bn of 
inventory provision loss in 3Q26, which reduced gross margin by 2pp. 
Excluding inventory provision loss, gross margin was c.35.8%, close to our 
previous estimate of 36.3%. 3Q26 revenue reached TWD197.1bn (up 8% 
QoQ and 31% YoY), which was also negatively affected by product 
shipment hiccups (vs. our previous estimate of 10% QoQ growth).

What we recommend: We trim our 2026-28E EPS by 2-6% to reflect lower 
revenue assumptions due to the impact of component shortages. We 
reaffirm our Buy (1) call but trim our 12M TP to TWD3,000 (from 
TWD3,088) based on an unchanged target PER of 49x (high-end of its 3-
year range of 11-50x) applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key 
downside risk: lower-than-expected demand.

How we differ: Our 2028E revenue is 12% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more bullish assumption regarding HVDC 
adoption.

29 October 2026

➢ Gross margin to recover on lower inventory provisions in 4Q26 
➢ Addressable market remains intact despite some near-term hiccups

Company profile 
Founded in 1971, Delta Electronics has been the global leader in switching power supply solutions since 
2002 and DC brushless fans since 2006. Its business covers 3 key areas: power electronics (eg, power 
supplies, thermal management products and passive components), automation (eg, controllers, robot 
arms and building management systems), and infrastructure (eg, datacentre infrastructure, networking 
and EV charging).

### Page 5

5

Delta Electronics (2308 TT): 29 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 30 Sep 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Delta's board has 7 non-independent and 5 independent directors with a term of three years.
The total number of directors and ratio of independent directors appear higher than most other
listed companies. Besides, according to Delta's CSR report, enhancing the board's functions is
one of the key goals for the company to achieve better corporate governance; also, the board
members' diversity and professionalism will be taken into consideration to ensure a good board
quality. Moreover, the company evaluates all the directors in the board using internal regulations
and an external independent institution each year and at least once every three years,
respectively. We believe Delta has implemented relatively more enhanced measure than most
listed firms to improve its board quality.
Capital management 2
During 2016-19, Delta's payout ratio was stable in the range of c.70%, but fell to 56% in 2020
and 53% in 2021. For 2020, it was mainly due to a sizeable non-cash re-valuation gain after the
acquisition of Delta Thailand (DET), while the company maintained more cash on hand to deal
with the uncertainties caused by COVID-19. However, the DPS growth from 2016 to 2021 was
limited, as Delta struggled with business diversification into new end-markets (eg, smartphone,
NEV). We believe that Delta's effort started to bear fruit in recent years after its NEV business
has gradually picked up. Overall, we see a stable dividend policy from Delta and expect its DPS
to continue to grow YoY in the future given that they declared a TWD11.6 DPS for 2025 which
equals a 50% payout ratio.
Related party &
transaction 1 Revenue from related parties stood at 12% of total 2025 revenue and purchases from relatedparties were at 11%. We see limited risk from related party transactions.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
Delta focuses on: 1) localised management, 2) supplier certification and risk management, and
3) supplier sustainability engagement as key initiatives under the scope of supply chain
management. In 2019, 144 new suppliers joined Delta's supply chain system. All of them passed
environmental and social evaluations, signed the declaration of "non-use of conflict materials",
and complied with Responsible Business Alliance's (BRA) Code of Conduct. Also, these vendors
had to implement and abide by Delta's supply chain CSR policy and code of conduct. To lower
ESG risks, Delta carries out RBA audits and monitors high-risk critical suppliers each year. The
suppliers that fail these audits have to provide detailed improvement plans in response to
identified issues. Besides, Delta has surveyed a total of 263 suppliers with potential risk and 63
suppliers with high risk of the total 1,331 suppliers, based on its CSR policy and code of conduct.
If these suppliers are flagged as high-risk and no concrete improvements are made, Delta may
consider terminating cooperation and transactions with them to improve the company's
engagement on supplier sustainability. Overall, we think Delta has a solid plan to improve its
supply chain management with specific KPIs, including: 1) achieving supplier improvement rate
of 80% in 2025, 2) reducing GHG emissions rate of critical suppliers by 10% in 2025, and 3)
improving water saving rate of critical suppliers by 12% in 2025.
S Data security 2
Delta's Information Security team created Delta's Information Security Policy in November 2013
and started providing educational training to global employees in 2014. Delta gradually
introduced the Information Security Management System (ISMS) in 2016 and obtained the ISO
27001 international certification in 2018. According to ISO27001, Delta has to conduct at least
one internal audit on information security and appoint an independent institution to conduct audit
externally. According to Delta, no major deficiencies have been found in recent years.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

1 群益投顧 
精測 (6510 TT) 前 三 季 大 賺 四 股 本。精 測 受 惠 於 HPC 測 試 需 求 ，3Q26 營 收 、獲 利 皆 創
單季新高， 4Q26 營收有望季增， 2027 年 展 望 正 向 ， 投 資 建 議 維 持Buy 。

研 究 員 ： 莊 禮 誠   
dan.chuang@cim.capital.com.tw

精測 3Q26 獲 利 季 增 、 年 增：  
測試介面大廠精測 10/27 公布 3Q26 營收 19.05 億元，
QoQ+16.17%/YoY+53.35% ；毛 利 率 57.3% ；營 益 率 35 .2 %；稅 後 純 益 5.51 億
元， QoQ+11.61%/YoY+100.03% ；稅後 EPS 16.75 元 。 前 三 季 歸 屬 母 公 司 業
主 合 併 淨 利 13.87 億 元 ， 年 增 逾 90% ， 每 股 純 益 42.2 元 ， 大 賺 逾 四 個 股 本 。 
精測 3Q26 營 收 、 獲 利 皆 創 單 季 新 高：  
精 測 表 示 ，3Q26 營 收 、 獲 利 等 財 務 指 標 均 創 單 季 新 高 ， 並 且 連 續 三 季 賺 逾
一 個 股 本，充 分 展 現 公 司 在 產 品 組 合、成 本 效 率 與 客 戶 需 求 的 競 爭 優 勢 持 續
強 化 ， 為 後 續 成 長 奠 定 穩 固 基 礎 。 
AI 帶 動 晶 片 朝 向高 效 能 、 高 頻 寬 與 高 功 率 架 構 發 展， 精 測 產 品 線 完 整：  
精 測 指 出 ， 隨 著 AI 應 用 全 面 擴 散 ， 推 動 晶 片 朝 高 效 能 、 高 頻 寬 與 高 功 率 架
構 發 展，測 試 複 雜 度 與 技 術 門 檻 同 步 提 高，客 戶 對 高 階 測 試 介 面 的 需 求 日 益
迫 切 ， 該 公 司 已 推 出 AI 與 高 效 能 運 算 （HPC ） 完 整 測 試 介 面 產 品 ， 涵 蓋 晶
圓 測 試 探 針 卡、高 功 率 老 化 測 試 板、IC 測 試 載 板 及 系 統 級 測 試 板 解 決 方 案，
以 滿 足 客 戶 一 次 購 足 的 需 求 。 
精測 完 整 導 入 智 慧 製 造 ， 在 產 能 擴 張 下 ， 有 助 維 持 獲 利 率：  
精 測 強 調 ， 公 司 同 步 精 進 研 發 與 製 造 流 程 ， 導 入AI 智 慧 製 造 ， 以 提 升 製 程
技 術 與 生 產 效 率，強 化 產 品 開 發 時 程 與 品 質 管 理 能 力，使 淨 利 率 在 產 能 擴 張
下 仍 穩 健 維 持 高 檔 水 準 。 
精測 4Q26 營運 審 慎 樂 觀 ：  
精 測 指 出，未 來 將 持 續 依 客 戶 需 求 與 市 場 變 化，審 慎 配 置 資 源，深 化 客 戶 合
作 關 係 。 研 發 重 點 聚 焦 下 一 世 代 探 針 卡 與 測 試 載 板 ， 以 因 應AI 客 製 化 晶 片
需 求 ， 同 時 推 動 「AI in House 」 企 業 智 慧 協 作 ， 探 索 AI 於 研 發 設 計 、 工 程
知 識 管 理 、 供 應 鏈 協 作 及 智 慧 製 造 等 應 用 ， 審 慎 樂 觀4Q26 及 未 來 營 運 。 
投 資 建 議 ：  
精 測 受 惠 於 HPC 測 試 需 求 ， 3Q26 營 收 、 獲 利 皆 創 單 季 新 高， 4Q26 營 收 有
望季增， 2027 年 展 望 正 向 ， 投 資 建 議 維 持 Buy 。

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

 현재주가 95,600원(10/30)

 동사의 3분기 실적은 매출액 31.6조원(+10.1%yoy), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.3%yoy, OPM 5.8%), 지배이익 1.29조원(-9.8%yoy)을 기록, 도매 판
매 증가로 인한 매출 증가에도 불구하고 인센티브/원자재 비용 증가로 인해 
컨센서스를 하회하는 실적 쇼크를 기록 
 영업이익 증감 요인: 인센티브&가격 -7,270억원, 믹스 -4,650억원, 재료비 
-3,680억원으로 총 1.56조원 비용 발생. 증가 요인은 판매량 +4,420억원, 
환율 +5,290억원, 기타비용 절감 +4,130억원으로 1.38조원 상쇄. 인센티
브로 인한 비용 증가 예상외로 컸는데 EV 판매량 증가 영향. 기말환율 하락
으로 판매보증충당금은 전년비 9,254억원 감소 
 전동화 믹스 증가: 글로벌 xEV 소매 판매 30만대(+47%yoy), 판매비중 
36.2%(+9.8%p yoy). HEV 17.2만대(+46%yoy), 텔루라이드, 스포티지 
HEV 공급 확대로 3분기 미국 HEV 판매 +118%yoy 기록. EV 12.3만대
(+75%yoy), 서유럽 EV 판매는 +163%yoy 기록 
 4분기 전망: 중동 분쟁 지속으로 원가 부담의 급격한 개선은 어려울 전망. 
연초 CID에서 제시한 연간 판매 목표는 달성 가능할 것으로 보이지만 기존 
수익성 목표를 하회할 전망. 중장기적으로 상품 경쟁력 강화, 지역별 파워트
레인 대응 전략 유지, 내부 원가 및 체질 개선으로 외부 비용 부담 상쇄를 
추진할 계획. 중동 분쟁 완화에 따른 비용 부담 해소와 HEV 비중 확대를 통
한 수익성 개선은 2027년 시점이 될 것으로 예상 
 단기 방향 전환 어려울 듯: 판매 호조에도 불구하고 저가형 EV 판매 비중 
증가함에 따라 수익성이 악화되는 모습이 3분기에도 재확인. 글로벌 수요 
변화를 감안했을 때, EV 시장 선점이 매우 중요한 것은 사실이지만 단기 수
익성 부담 커지고 있는 점 우려됨. 중국산 EV의 글로벌 확산, 중동 전쟁 장
기화에 따른 원가 부담 증대가 연말까지는 영향을 미칠 것. 동사에 대한 투
자의견 매수와 목표주가 16만원 유지함

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
좋은 실적이 상수인 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다. 이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 
이전 전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 26 년 3분기 
영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 상회한 
수준이다. 
실적이 끌고, 수급이 밀고 
지금까지는 높은 실적에 대한 불안보다는 먼 미래에 대한 불확실성을 앞서 주가에 
반영시키는 힘이 더 강하게 작용하다가, 주가가 1,190,000원을 찍은 이후 상승세를 
보이고 있다. 실적을 발표한 10월 28일도 10% 넘게 상승했다. 기대 이상의 실적과 
미국 반도체 시장 분위기가 좋았던 영향이다. 앞으로는 악재에 대해서 민감도가 떨
어지는 구간에 진입할 것으로 전망한다. 지나치게 하락한 주가와 밸류에이션이 버팀
목이 될 것이고, Anthropic의 IPO까지 발표되는 Big Tech 기업들의 실적은 시장의 
우려를 충분히 상쇄할 수 있을 것으로 기대한다. 추가로 진행되는 자사주 매수도 불
안한 수급을 해소할 수 있을 전망이다. 
목표주가는 괴리율을 고려해서 하향 조정 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 26년에도 AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 
시장은 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 
때문이다. 26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 
판단한다. 목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 
업황에 대한 우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 27년 EPS 
538,187원에 PER 6배를 적용하였다.

### Page 2

SK하이닉스 (000660)

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기, 모든 악재를 잠재운 실적 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다.  이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 이전 
전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 
DRAM 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 35.4% 증가했다. B/G 는 +11.5%, ASP 는 
+28.2%로 추정했다. 3분기 DRAM 가격 상승률은 경쟁사 대비 높은 수준이다. Server 
DRAM 비중이 2분기 대비 높아진 것과 고객 믹스가 개선된 영향으로 추정한다. 
HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정한다. HBM이 3분기 가격에 미친 영향은 
제한적인 수준으로 판단한다.  
NAND 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 18.2% 증가했다. B/G 는 -1%, ASP는 +26%로 
추정한다. 이전 전망치를 상회했다. SSD 비중은 2분기 75%에서 3분기에는 77%로 
상승했다.  
26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 
상회한 수준이다. DRAM 영업이익은 지난 분기 대비 42.3% 증가한 65.1조원으로 
예상한다. 영업이익률은 전 분기 대비 4.0%p 상승한 83.0%로 추정한다. NAND 
영업이익은 전 분기 대비 23.1% 증가한 18.2조원으로 추정한다. 영업이익률은 전 
분기 대비 2.9%p 높아진 72.4%로 추정한다.

4 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 4분기, DRAM 물량 효과 
SK하이닉스의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 29.4% 증가한 135.3조원으로 
예상한다. 가격상승은 지속될 것으로 예상하지만 26년 3분기 대비 상승폭이 낮아질 
전망이다. 물량은 3분기 대비 25% 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 신규 라인 가동 영향이다. 
4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 34.1% 증가한 111.7조원으로 예상한다. DRAM 
90.9조원, NAND 20.7조원이다.  
DRAM 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 36.4% 증가한 107조원으로 예상한다. ASP 는 
+15%, Bit Growth는 +25.0%로 가정하였다. HBM 출하는 지난 분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 예상하고, HBM4 비중은 60%대로 높아질 전망이다. 
26년 4분기 NAND 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 11.6% 증가할 것으로 예상한다. Bit 
Growth는 +5%로 전망하고, ASP는 +12%로 예상한다. SSD 비중은 소폭 개선될 
전망이다. SSD 내에서 eSSD 비중은 90% 수준이다.

IBKS RESEARCH │5 
공정 전환 속도는 예상보다 빠르게 진행 중: 1cb nm, 321L 
SK하이닉스는 1c nm 비중을 빠르게 확대하고 있다. Wafer 투입이 제한된 상황에서 
공급 확대를 위한 최선의 방법이기 때문이다. HBM 수요는 1b nm를 중심으로 대응할 
예정이다. 이와 관련된 물량도 꾸준히 증가하지만, 출하 Bit 기준으로 1c nm 비중이 
26년 1분기 3% 수준에서 26년 3분기에는 20%, 연말에는 33%까지 높아질 전망이다. 
HBM이 아닌 DRAM 물량이 크게 증가할 전망이다. 이를 통해서 높아진 DRAM 수요 
대응할 것으로 예상한다. 26년 출하 Bit Growth는 28.5%로 상향되었다. 
NAND 생산라인의 공정 전환은 High-End로 꾸준히 변화 중이다. 321L 는 26년 
2분기에는 176L와 Cross over 되었다. 예상보다 1분기 빠른 속도다. 26년 3분기 321L 
비중은 50%이다. 26년말까지 321L를 64%까지 확대할 계획이다.

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
Check Point 
1. DRAM ASP의 차별화 
26년 3분기 DRAM ASP는 메이커별로 차별화가 크다. 26년 2분기 대비 상승폭은 마
이크론이 10% 후반, 삼성전자는 20% 초반으로 추정하고, SK하이닉스는 20% 후반이
다. LTA, HBM 등 다양한 변수가 있지만 SK하이닉스는 고객 비중 변화가 가장 큰 변
수라고 설명한다.  
2. HBM4 물량, 수율 문제 
26년 3분기에 가장 시끄러웠던 변수가 SK하이닉스의 HBM4 관련 품질 문제이다. 26
년 3분기 DRAM 매출과 영업이익으로는 문제가 있었다고 판단하기 어렵다. 26년 3분
기 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정되고 4분기 말에는 비중이 60%
까지 높아질 것으로 전망한다.   
3. HBM 8Hi 확대가 시장 규모 감소는 아니다. 
HBM4는 12Hi가 원래 제품 사양이다. HBM 공급이 부족해지면서 GPU를 좀 더 많이 
팔기 위해서 적용되는 HBM 용량을 줄이는 방향으로 제품 설계가 변경되는 중이다. 
2027년 하반기에는 8Hi 비중이 12Hi 비중을 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 하지만 이로 
인해서 HBM 전체 물량이 감소하는 것은 아닌 것으로 전망하고 있다.  
4. 투자와 생산은 다르다 
SK하이닉스는 용인팹을 공기를 앞당겨서 완공할 계획이다. 이로 인해서 공급량이 예
상 대비 커지는 것에 대한 우려가 있지만 건물 완공과 장비 반입에는 시차가 있고, 수
요를 고려하지 않은 장비 반입은 없을 것으로 밝혔다. 
5. 주주환원. 더 남아 있다 
공시를 통해서 26년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원의 주주환원 계획을 밝혔다. 배당을 30
조원 이상, 자사주 매입/소각을 60조원이상을 추진할 계획이다. 26년 4분기 중에 배
당은 14조원을 집행하고, 자사주는 20조원을 매입할 예정이다. 자사주 20조원 중에서 
10조원은 종업원 상여금 지급용이어서 주주환원에 포함되지 않는다.  
26년 4분기 실적 발표에서 추가적인 배당과 자사주 매입에 대한 계획을 발표할 것으
로 예상한다.

ASP 움직임은 메이커별로

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
펀더멘탈과 센티멘탈의 줄다리기 구간 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 시장은 
성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 
26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 판단한다.  
목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 업황에 대한 
우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 2027년 EPS 538,187원에 PER 
6배를 적용하였다.  
아직 제대로 보급되지 않은 Agent AI가 확산되면 컴퓨팅 파워 필요성에 대한 인식도 
크게 바뀔 것으로 전망한다. 메타의 MUSE는 가입자당 8GB의 DRAM을 가정하고 
있다. Open AI, Google, Anthropic 에서도 Agent Platform이 공개된다면 필요한 
DRAM은 폭발적으로 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 
메모리 수요에 대한 규모가 너무 커지는 것이 걱정이지 누가 살 것인지에 대한 것은 
걱정할 문제가 아니라 판단한다. 기존 AI 모델의 진화, 효율적 모델의 등장, Agent AI 
모델의 확대 모두 메모리 수요를 기하급수적으로 확장시키는 변수이다. 
투자 주체들의 자금난이 수요를 저해하는 변수는 아닌 것으로 판단한다. 대체 
투자자들은 지속적으로 증가할 것으로 전망하기 때문이다.  
메모리 업체들의 투자 계획은 수요에 대응하기 위한 투자일 뿐 공급과잉을 만들 
수준은 아닐 것으로 판단한다.

동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

Document 243: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로긴 호흡으로 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로긴 호흡으로 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-29
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

(005930) 
삼성전자 
2026.09.29

반도체 손인준 
6154 / sij@eugenefn.com

긴 호흡으로 
 투자의견 STRONG BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 560,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 270,000원(9/28)

 삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익이 103.6조원(+16%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 메모리 가격 등 전반적인 반도체 업황에 대한 기존 가정은 대체로 
유지했으나, 최근 원화 강세에 따라 원/달러 환율 가정을 1,440원에서 
1,400원으로 낮추고 MX 사업부의 적자 확대를 반영해 실적 추정치를 하
향 조정했다. 또한 2Q26 재고가 3Q26 매출로 인식되는 과정에서 발생하
는 추가 성과급 충당금 부담도 반영했다.  
 DS 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 104.0조원(+17%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 서버 DRAM과 eSSD 중심의 가격 인상이 이어지며, DRAM과 
NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +17%, +18%QoQ 상승할 것으로 추정한
다. 1cnm 수율 개선과 HBM4의 빠른 램프업도 실적 개선에 기여할 전망이
다. Foundry/LSI는 HBM4 베이스다이 생산 확대, Groq 3 LPU 양산 및 선
단 공정 가격 인상에 힘입어 하반기 매출 성장이 예상된다. 다만 성과급 충
당금과 감가상각비 부담으로 영업적자 축소 폭은 제한적일 것으로 보인다. 
 DX 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 -1.8조원(적자지속qoq)을 기록할 것으로 추
정한다. 삼성전자는 메모리 가격 급등에도 제품 가격 인상을 제한하며 시장 
점유율 확대에 주력하고 있다. 이러한 가격 정책과 갤럭시 Z 폴드8 등 신제
품 흥행에 힘입어 판매량은 당초 예상치를 상회할 것으로 보인다. 다만 원
가 상승분의 전가가 제한되면서 영업적자 폭은 크게 확대될 전망이다.  
 삼성전자의 4Q26 영업이익은 114.9조원(+11%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. DRAM과 NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +11%, +12%QoQ 상승
하며 견조한 흐름을 이어갈 것으로 보인다. 환율 가정 조정에 따라 2026년
과 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 각각 365.2조원(+735%yoy), 595.8조원
(+63%YoY)으로 낮췄다. 4Q26~1Q27 범용 DRAM·NAND 및 2027년 
HBM 가격 협상 환경은 공급업체에 유리하게 전개되고 있어, 향후 가격 가
정의 추가 상향 가능성이 높다고 판단한다.  
 극심한 DRAM 공급 부족으로 인한 HBM 적층 단수 하향 조정(base die 수
요 증가), Memory Wall에 대응하기 위한 3D Stacked DRAM·zHBM 도입 
논의 등은 파운드리의 기술적 가치를 부각시키는 요인이다. 파운드리의 수
익성 회복 속도는 완만할 전망이지만, 메모리와 파운드리를 연계한 턴키 비
즈니스의 시너지는 AI 반도체 시장 내 경쟁력을 높이고, 메모리 시장 점유
율 확대에도 기여할 것으로 보인다. 적극적인 메모리 가격 인상 정책으로 
업계 최고 수준의 Blended ASP도 장기간 유지할 것으로 예상한다. 
 이제 메모리 업종 주가는 업황의 지속 가능성을 확인하고 꾸준한 주주환원
을 반영하며, 중장기적으로 점진적인 상승세를 이어갈 것으로 판단한다. 삼
성전자는 메모리·파운드리를 아우르는 턴키 역량과 가격 정책에 기반한 실
적 차별화를 통해 피어 그룹 내 경쟁력을 높여갈 것으로 보인다.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

Document 277: [402340] SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK하이닉스 최대주주 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[402340] SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK하이닉스 최대주주 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
SK 하이닉스 최대주주 
SK 하이닉스 4 분기도 배당 주당 20,000 원 이상  
SK 하이닉스는 전일 주당 20,000원(실적반영: 10,000원, 키옥시아 특별배당: 
10,000원)의 3분기 배당을 발표하였다. 키옥시아 특별배당은 3분기 배당과 26년 회
계연도 결산배당으로 나누어 지급예정이다. 이에 SK스퀘어는 2조 9,220억원(1분기
와 2분기 배당수입은 각 548억원)의 배당수입을 수취하게 된다. SK하이닉스는 26년 
전체 현금 배당 규모는 30조원 이상을 발표했는데, 3분기까지 지급하는 배당금은 14.6
조원이기 때문에 4분기에도 최소한 주당 20.000원 이상의 배당이 예상된다. 이 경우 
SK 스퀘어의 26 년 배당수입은 6 조원에 달할 것으로 예상한다. SK 스퀘어는 ‘26년
~’28년 경상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 주주환원정책을 보유하고 있
어 SK스퀘어의 현 시가총액 대비 27년 주주환원율은 최소 1.5% 이상 될 전망이다.

SK 하이닉스 자사주 취득·소각으로 높아질 지배력 
SK하이닉스는 20조원의 자사주 매입을 발표하였다. 이 중 10조원은 임직원 보상목적
이며, 10조원은 주주환원 목적이다. 26년 총 60조원 이상의 자사주 취득·소각 예정(8
월 결의분 40 조원 포함)으로 임직원 보상 목적의 자사주 매입은 제외되기 때문에 26
년 실적 확정 후 추가적으로 10조원 이상의 자사주 취득이 예상된다. 기 취득한 자사주 
40조원(3.3%)는 11월 5일 소각 예정으로 소각 이후 SK스퀘어의 SK하이닉스 지분
율은 20.5%(2 분기말 기준)에서 21.2%로 상승하게 된다. 이번 발표한 10 조원
(1.0%) 자사주 소각시 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 21.4%이 되며, 추가로 10 조원을 추가 
소각한다면 21.7%까지 지분율이 상승하게 된다.

SK 하이닉스 대비 높은 베타 
SK스퀘어에 대한 투자의견 매수를 유지한다. SK스퀘어 NAV의 98%가량을 차지하
는 SK하이닉스의 현금배당 증가로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 재원이 확대되고, SK하이닉
스의 자사주 취득·소각으로 지배력이 강화되기 때문이다. SK하이닉스는 전일 컨퍼런스 
콜을 통해 29년까지 D램과 HBM 물량이 사실상 모두 솔드아웃된 상황이라 밝히며 주
가 반등 가능성이 높은데, SK 스퀘어 베타(1.8)는 SK 하이닉스(1.7) 대비 높기 때문에 
SK 하이닉스 주가 반등에 따른 SK 스퀘어의 동반 주가 상승을 기대할 수 있다. 현재 
SK스퀘어의 NAV 대비 할인율은 46.5%이며, SK하이닉스가 자사주60조원 소각시 
할인율은 49.4%까지 상승하게 된다.

2026-10-29

지주회사/통신서비스/ESG. 최관순 / ks1.choi@sks.co.kr / 3773-8812

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 1,850,000 원(유지) 
현재주가: 828,000 원 
상승여력: 123.4%

Signal: SK 하이닉스 현금배당 확대로 SK 스퀘어 주주환원 재원 증가 
Key: SK 하이닉스 자사주 소각으로 SK 스퀘어 지배력 확대

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다.

Document 330: Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

13026023     1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers 
09 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained very solid in September. Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), or 5.1% excluding gasoline, both the third strongest growth rates over the past four 
years. Month-over-month (MoM) spending was more muted, especially without gas. 
• Spending shows considerable breadth: the vast majority of discretionary retail and services categories are showing growth. 
Moreover, this strength extends across income cohorts, with the ongoing recovery of lower- and middle-income households' 
spending growth. 
• With the winter months fast approaching, holiday spending is already increasing at a solid rate, though it's still early. In Bank of 
America deposit data through September, buy now, pay later (BNPL) usage is rising but not at a rate that is overly concerning. 
However, nearly one in every five households that use BNPL has a repayment of over $500.

Consumer Checkpoint is a regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It aims to provide a holistic and real-time estimate of US 
consumers’ spending and their financial well-being, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America proprietary data. Such data is not 
intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, financial conditions or performance of 
Bank of America. 
A solid September for spending   
Consumer spending continued to look solid in September. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), while spending excluding gasoline increased 5.1% YoY. Notably, both are the third 
strongest growth rates in the past four years. Seasonally-adjusted total card spending rose 0.2% month-over-month (MoM), 
while spending excluding gasoline was little changed. Overall, spending growth remains well above the 2025 average rate 
(Exhibit 1). 
Exhibit 1: Total card spending per household rose 6.2% YoY and 5.1% YoY excluding gasoline in September 
Total card spending growth per household, based on Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card data (monthly, MoM%, seasonally adjusted (SA)) and 
(monthly, YoY%, non-SA, right-hand side (rhs))

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

### Page 2

  I NSTI TUTE

Most spending categories are growing  
One striking thing about recent card spending, in our view, is that it is well balanced across categories. For example, both 
spending on retail (excluding gasoline) and services grew strongly YoY in September, at 3.5% and 5.4%, respectively. Meanwhile, 
retail spending is showing notably better momentum than in 2025 (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: Spending is rising across retail and services 
Retail (excluding gasoline) and services card spending per household (monthly, seasonally adjusted, index January 2025 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Well balanced across “nice-to-have” and “need-to-have” retail categories  
Is the momentum in retail spending reflective of “nice-to-have” discretionary or “need-to-have” necessity spending? In retail, 
this is a nuanced question, as many categories sit in a gray area between “necessity” and “discretionary”. General merchandise 
stores, for example, sell both essentials like groceries as well as nice-to-have electronics or even jewelry.  
In Exhibit 3, we account for this by grouping retail spending into “more discretionary” (furniture, electronics, building materials, 
online retailers, clothing retailers and miscellaneous stores), “partly discretionary” (auto, health and personal care retailers, 
general merchandise) and “mostly necessity” (gas and groceries). Even within these categories the edges will be blurred, but the 
overall impression is that retail spending growth is widely distributed, with spending rising across all fronts. One caveat: “mostly 
discretionary” spending has been making a smaller contribution since June. 
Exhibit 3: Retail spending is distributed across discretionary and necessity categories  
Percentage points contribution to YoY growth by type of retail spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data. See methodology for category aggregation definitions.  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Looking at the share of “more discretionary” retail spending categories that are increasing YoY underscores that growth is well 
balanced and broad-based (Exhibit 4). Non-store and online retailers and miscellaneous stores saw some of the fastest growth, 
while furniture and building materials were among the laggards, though other durable categories showed some strength.

While some of this spending growth likely reflects the impact of higher prices pushing up spending across a swathe of 
categories, looking just at transactions as a proxy for volumes shows a similar picture, with only slightly less diffusion (Exhibit 5).

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

For services, growth is driven mostly by discretionary spending  
In services spending, on the other hand, the boundaries between discretionary and necessity spending are often clearer. Travel 
spending (e.g., airlines, lodging) can be thought of as, by and large, discretionary. At the same time, utility bills, rent and 
insurance payments are necessities.  
And in services, discretionary spending remains the clear driver, accounting for over three-quarters of YoY spending growth in 
September (Exhibit 6). Leisure and housing services are major components of growth, though travel and restaurants also made 
positive contributions. However, as we noted last month strength in airline spending largely reflects price rises rather than 
transactions growth (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling).  
Exhibit 6: Services spending growth has been mostly driven by discretionary spending over the past year 
Percentage points contribution to growth by type of services spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data

Lower-income households’ spending outpaced all but the top 5% 
The breadth in spending growth across categories is also evident across income cohorts. In September, lower-income 
households’ total card spending growth was 5.5% YoY, while the equivalent figures for middle- and higher-income households 
were 4.9% and 4.8% YoY, respectively. The top 5% of households by income continue to show the fastest spending growth, 
5.9% in September, though this has eased somewhat (Exhibit 7). 
Overall, the divergences across income cohorts are much smaller than they were in 2025 and the first three months of this year. 
Just focusing on spending excluding key necessities (gasoline, food and utilities) doesn’t meaningfully change the picture of 
convergence across income cohorts (Exhibit 8).  
Exhibit 7: The top 5% saw the strongest spending growth of any 
income cohort… 
Total card spending per household by income tercile and the top 5% 
(three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

some since June 
Discretionary card spending per household by income tercile and the top 
5% (three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The strength in lower- and middle-income households’ discretionary spending is occurring despite necessity spending shifting 
from a tailwind to a headwind over the past year amid rising gas prices (Exhibit 9). In our view, a key component of why these 
households have been able to combine rising necessity spending with strength in discretionary is the labor market and a 
recovery in their after-tax wage growth (Exhibit 10).  
In September, lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY from 4.7% YoY in August, while growth for middle- and higher-
income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. These changes were modest, and lower-income households are 
continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” pattern we 
observed over much of 2025 and 2026.

Should we be concerned about the easing in household spending and after-tax wage growth for those with the top 5% of 
incomes? For now, probably not, as in our view this spending growth is likely being driven by wealth effects rather than wage 
gains. Absent a sharp correction in equity markets, there is little reason to expect them to retrench their spending. However, 
developments here are worth watching as this cohort comprises a significant proportion of overall consumer spending. They also 
tend to make outsized contributions to several discretionary spending categories (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: The top 5% of households by income make up a disproportionate amount of spending in highly discretionary categories 
Share of spending by the top 5% of households by income across select discretionary categories (three-month moving average to September 2026, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
With summer gone, thoughts turn to the holidays and BNPL… 
Now that summer is over, the next big gauge of consumer momentum is how people spend during the run-up to, and over, the 
holiday season. It is early days, but September spending on holiday items ran well ahead of 2025 (Exhibit 12). Given the backdrop 
of firmer after-tax wage growth and breadth across retail and services categories, this is perhaps not surprising. However, some 
of this spending strength could be due to rising prices as opposed to more purchases.

A concern often raised as we move into the holidays is whether consumers will lean on less traditional forms of credit such as 
buy now, pay later (BNPL) to finance their spending. Frequent BNPL users tend to have higher credit card utilization rates (read 
more in: Do consumers have wiggle room to absorb higher gas prices?).  
Looking at the share of households in Bank of America deposit data with a BNPL payment, we do tend to see a seasonal rise 
over the holidays (Exhibit 13), though in past years, these increases have quickly reversed. Further, while the underlying trend in 
BNPL usage is upwards, there are no signs of a sharp acceleration. We do, however, see some evidence that BNPL is being used 
to finance more expensive purchases, with the share of households making repayments above $500 rising, albeit relatively slowly 
(Exhibit 14).  
Exhibit 13: 8.3% of Bank of America households had a BNPL 
payment in September

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The tendency towards greater usage is occurring across income cohorts, though is somewhat more pronounced for lower-
income households (Exhibit 15). However, higher-income households have the largest share of BNPL payments above $500 
(Exhibit 16). Overall, in our view, the increase in the share of BNPL users heading into the season does not appear too 
concerning, though the rate of usage for some BNPL consumers does bear watching.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• We've lifted our Q3 expectation for real PCE on incoming data and a slightly firmer consumer
backdrop in the wake of recent revisions.
Consumer spending looks firmer in the third quarter, and we now expect real PCE to rise at a 3.4%
annualized clip. This upward revision reflects recent data, with August consumption coming in firm
with real spending rising by the fastest monthly clip in nearly two years, but also reflects a slightly

firmer consumer backdrop in the wake of the BEA's annual revisions released late September. Although
revisions did not materially alter spending, they lifted the level of household income in recent years,
which also boosted the personal saving rate. The trajectory of real income growth remains weak, but
the consumer still looks to be in a slightly stronger financial position than previously believed, which
helps explain recent resilience in spending and suggests consumers may have a bit more runway ahead.

Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products and Inventories
• The AI build out will lead to another surge in business fixed investment spending in Q3, though the
effect on headline growth will be partly offset by stronger imports.
Business fixed investment spending continues to be supported primarily by the high-tech buildout.
We forecast real equipment investment to rise at around a 16% annualized clip in Q3, but part of
this boost to headline GDP growth will be offset by stronger import growth that is facilitating the

build out. Net imports of computers are tracking to surge in the third quarter, which will provide a
sizable lift to information processing equipment investment, or around half of overall equipment.
And while non-defense capital goods shipments (including aircraft) slipped in August, the near-term
growth trajectory remains supportive of a pickup in equipment. The rebuild in inventories we have
been anticipating is also tracking to be a bit stronger as businesses replenish drawn down product.

Investment: Residential
• The abrupt rise in mortgage rates is likely to dampen residential investment over the next few
quarters. Residential spending should find support next year as longer-term interest rates
moderate somewhat.
We are unlikely to see another positive residential investment reading in Q3. Rising mortgage rates

have sent a chill over builder outlooks, pushing sales expectations to their weakest level in more than
three years. While builders have responded with deeper price cuts, affordability pressures continue
to weigh on single-family sales and construction outlays. Apartment construction is simultaneously
soft amid rising financing costs and modest rent growth, and home improvement spending remains
constrained by high materials costs and interest rates. While residential investment is likely to remain

4 | Economics

### Page 5

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
a drag this year, we expect a slight moderation in longer-term interest rates in 2027, which would
provide a more supportive backdrop.
Investment: Nonresidential Structures
• We have not made material changes to our nonresidential structures forecast. Despite elevated
interest rates, data center and related spending should continue to support structures investment
through our forecast horizon.
Nonresidential structures spending has accelerated sharply, overwhelmingly driven by data centers

and electric power facilities. As data center construction expands geographically and demand rises
for semiconductors, high-tech capex has also started to generate positive spillovers for warehousing
and manufacturing construction. This buildout, which appears relatively insensitive to interest rates,
should be enough to sustain solid growth in structures investment even as spending across most
other categories remains essentially flat. Domestic energy is another green shoot, with elevated prices

supporting ongoing growth in active rotary rigs.

Net Exports & Trade
• An expected drag from trade in Q3 got larger in light of a near-record trade deficit in August, net
exports is now expected to lop off three full percentage points from third quarter growth.

Exports increased in August for the first time in four months, but the increase was more than offset by
an even larger increase in imports. The upshot was a widening in the trade deficit to a near-record gap
of more than $105 billion. Going into the report, we had a 1.9 percentage point drag from net exports
penciled in for Q3 GDP; we revised that to a full 3.0 percentage points drag in light of the record gap.
Even a modest narrowing in the final months of the year would likely mean a lift to headline GDP in

Q4, but the real story here is that trade will remain a headwind throughout the forecast period owing
largely to surging imports of tech-related products amid the AI boom.
Labor Market
• The jobs market remains stable. We continue to expect the unemployment rate to end the year at
4.2% and hold near that level in 2027.
We modestly revised down our near-term payroll growth forecast following the September jobs
report, but otherwise we have left our labor market outlook unchanged. Measures of labor demand

growth continue to point to a stable labor market. Job openings have inched higher on a year-ago
basis but remain broadly steady while initial jobless claims, WARN notices and Challenger job cut
announcements continue to signal low layoffs. Labor force participation appears to be improving after
its sharp pullback in the first half of the year. Looking ahead, we expect payroll growth to average
around 60K per month in 2027, roughly in line with our estimate of breakeven job growth. That should

Economics | 5

### Page 6

Monthly Economics
keep the unemployment rate near 4.2% in 2027 and leave labor market conditions broadly consistent
with "full employment." Notably, our expectation for labor cost growth of ~3.4% is the reason we
continue to reiterate that labor cost growth is not a contributor to the excess inflation seen in the
data.
Inflation
• We expect the core PCE deflator to rise 3.2% and 2.6% Q4/Q4 in 2026 and 2027, respectively.
Headline inflation remains heavily influenced by developments in the Middle East, with higher oil

prices lifting gasoline and diesel prices. Food inflation has been tame this year but looks poised to
strengthen as higher transportation and commodity prices work their way through supply chains.
Beyond food and energy, price pressures remain concentrated in a handful of categories, most notably
airfares and AI-related tech goods. Core goods inflation is showing signs of stabilizing, as waning tariff-
related price pressures have been offset by resilient demand tied to the AI buildout. We expect some

improvement here in 2027. Still-low shelter inflation and cooler price growth for airfares and health
care should help bring about some moderation in core services inflation next year. We expect the core
PCE deflator to rise 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis in 2026 before slowing to 2.6% in 2027.

Fiscal Policy
• Fiscal year (FY) 2026 ended on September 30, and the federal government tallied a $2.0 trillion
budget deficit (6.5% of GDP). We project the deficit to be unchanged in dollar terms in FY 2027

and a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).
• Another continuing resolution to extend government funding past the December 11 deadline
seems like the most likely outcome at this time for the FY 2027 budget. It is possible Republicans
use the lame duck session of Congress to increase defense spending and raise the debt ceiling.
Looking ahead to the lame duck session of Congress that will follow the midterm elections, Congress
needs to pass a bill that extends government funding past December 11 to avert a government

shutdown. Another continuing resolution that punts the issue into Q1 seems like the most likely
outcome. Perhaps more interesting will be whether Republicans attempt to use budget reconciliation
to pass one more partisan piece of legislation. If they do, we suspect the two things at the top the
agenda will be more funding for national defense and immigration enforcement priorities as well as a
debt ceiling increase. For the latter, raising it in the lame duck would remove some leverage Democrats

could have in 2027 if they recapture one or both chambers. It is still very early, but we estimate the
debt ceiling "X date" by which Congress must act or face default is sometime in late Q3/early Q4 of
next year.

### Page 7

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• Our base case forecast is for the FOMC to hike the fed funds rate by 25 bps in December and
then keep the policy rate unchanged in 2027 at 4.00%-4.25%, with risks skewed toward additional
tightening.
As we go to print, the FOMC seems more likely than not to keep the fed funds rate unchanged in
October. But given the rationale under which the Fed started this tightening cycle and the forcefulness

with which Warsh has driven home his displeasure with elevated inflation, it seems entirely plausible
to us that October will be a live meeting. At a minimum, the recent inflation data seem to make a hike
in December a lock. Past December, the outlook will be highly dependent on future inflation data. Our
expectation is that core inflation will cool gradually in 2027, and we still do not see signs of demand-
side inflation in the data. But hiking cycles have an inertial component to them, and the recent inflation

data reset the clock to zero on the Fed seeing several good inflation prints in a row. This creates upside
risk to our base case for the fed funds rate.

International Developments
• Global growth remains resilient, leaving our 2026 and 2027 forecasts unchanged. Our inflation
forecasts are also unchanged, although risks of delayed price pass-through could prolong inflation
pressures into 2027.

• Monetary policy continues to lean toward tightening across advanced economies, while emerging
market central banks are increasingly cautious, either holding rates or easing more gradually as
inflation pressures rebuild.
The balance of risks to global inflation remains tilted to the upside, complicating the monetary policy
outlook even as growth has proven more resilient than expected. Elevated energy prices, particularly
for refined petroleum products, persistent geopolitical uncertainty and El Niño-related food price

risks could prolong inflationary pressures into 2027. While second-round effects still remain relatively
contained, repeated supply shocks are testing central banks’ willingness to look through the temporary
price increases, particularly where inflation expectations remain elevated, even as higher government
bond yields are already contributing to tighter financial conditions.
Across advanced economies, central banks face increasingly difficult trade-offs as persistent inflation

pressures limit the scope for policy accommodation. The Eurozone remains particularly vulnerable
to energy supply disruptions, with elevated natural gas prices and low storage levels adding to
inflation risks. We continue to expect a 25 bps European Central Bank hike in December, followed by a
prolonged hold through 2027. Fiscal concerns are also adding to pressure, particularly in France, where
the selloff in government debt has pushed yields higher. In the U.K., resilient activity and renewed

Economics | 7

### Page 8

Monthly Economics
inflation pressures similarly underpin our expectation for a Bank of England hike in November,
followed by a hold through 2027.
Elsewhere, policy paths are becoming more differentiated. After four hikes this year brought the Cash
Rate to 4.60%, we expect the Reserve Bank of Australia to remain on hold, although renewed inflation
pressures or stronger-than-expected activity could prompt further tightening. We also still expect
the Bank of Canada to hold at 2.25%, with subdued growth weighing against energy-driven inflation

risks, although incoming inflation data over the coming weeks will be critical in determining whether
persistent price pressures warrant tightening monetary policy perhaps as early as the December
meeting. Japan remains an exception to the broader hold outlook: while the yen's recovery provides
some relief from imported inflation, elevated energy prices and import dependence continue to pose
upside risks. We expect the Bank of Japan to hike again by year-end and now expect an additional 25

bps hike in Q3-2027, bringing the terminal rate to 1.75%.
The emerging market story has increasingly shifted from easing toward patience or selective
tightening. In Asia, China's weak domestic demand and persistent property-sector strains continue to
warrant policy support, with AI-related exports providing some offset. We expect a more gradual pace
of Reserve Requirement Ratio cuts alongside targeted monetary and fiscal measures. India faces the

opposite challenge, with elevated food and energy prices and rupee depreciation strengthening the
case for further Reserve Bank of India tightening. In Latin America, peso depreciation and elevated
energy costs pose upside inflation risks for Mexico, although limited exchange-rate pass-through
should allow Banxico to remain on hold while policymakers monitor underlying inflation and inflation
expectations. In Brazil, election-related uncertainty and renewed inflation pressures are likely to delay

further easing, with cuts expected to resume in Q1-2027 once policymakers see more convincing
evidence of disinflation.

Eurozone: Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer

confidence falls back
02/10/2026 3
We have selected two inflation measures (with and without energy) and six survey indicators: 1/ & 2/ business confidence, as
measured by the PMIs in the manufacturing and services sectors; 3/ & 4/ input and output prices components of the
composite PMI (in order to identify direct inflationary pressures); 5/ the suppliers’ delivery times component of the
manufacturing PMI (a direct indicator of possible supply difficulties and supply-demand imbalance, and therefore, indirectly,

of inflationary pressures in the making); and 6/ consumer confidence (measured by the “assessment of financial situation in
the next 12 months” in order to capture the impact of inflation on purchasing power). The trends in each of these indicators
are observed relative to month m=0, corresponding to the start of the conflict. Each line does not represent the level of the
indicator, but its cumulative variation compared to month m=0.
The overall picture from the confidence surveys available for September is globally positive. According to the PMIs, the

business climate in services has indeed seen a clear improvement, and that in the manufacturing sector remains comfortably
in expansion territory for the 8 th month in a row. However, consumer confidence has recorded its first decline in 5 months,
likely driven by rising inflation and concerns over purchasing power. News on the inflation front is negative. PMI surveys
report a strengthening of inflationary pressures: this trend is not a surprise given the tensions in energy prices, but it is

nonetheless significant. As for inflation itself, it continues to rise, more markedly in September: +0.6 percentage points, to
3.8% year-on-year (Eurostat flash estimate), of which 0.4 pp du to the higher energy contribution. Inflation excluding energy
increased as well, by 0.2 pp to 2.3% (of which 0.1 pp due to the rise in food inflation).
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas

Document 448: Top of Mind_ Higher bond yields_ how concerning_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind_ Higher bond yields_ how concerning_.pdf

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Global bond yields have risen to multi-decade highs, yet growth and markets have
remained remarkably resilient. But with some pressures beginning to emerge (e.g. in
European sovereign bond markets), can this resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities
be revealed? We ask the IMF’s Tobias Adrian, Moody’s Analytics’ Mark Zandi, and GS
economists. Adrian is relatively sanguine about the economy’s resilience, though
notes downside risks, while GS economists expect only a modest growth drag from

higher rates, but a more consequential one if yields remain near current or higher
levels. Zandi is generally more concerned, describing the US economy as vulnerable
rather than resilient, and sees one-in-three odds of the economy going off the rails.
Meanwhile, GS strategists and analysts are not especially concerned about banks or
markets more broadly, but warn that further increases in yields or rate volatility could
test risk assets. Key to watch: whether the increasingly interconnected and leveraged

AI investment boom can continue to withstand higher rates.
Tobias Adrian, Special Advisor and former Director of Monetary and Capital Markets, IMF
“The global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, provided they do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same
time, I do worry about downside risks.”
Mark Zandi, Chief Economist, Moody’s Analytics
